#79842 24 2005-06-30T04:59:37Z KinGAleX
Sporks:

The sequence of letters, "Spork," now used unanimously for the cuttle* all we Earth people use in those dire circumstances in which a spoon, or fork for that matter, just won't do. It actually comes from the Greek root word "Sporak," meaning "Ease of dessert eating." It is only those huge global corporate companies and their marketing divisions, who have decided against the elegancy of this definition to fall to a new all-time low, thus forgetting our English language's Greek roots, and settling with "Spork" as a blend of spoon and fork. Whether we actually need them or not is negated by the want of modern culture to make everything, and yes, I mean everything, much quicker. Sex, cleaning, downloading times. If you don't believe me, rent out 8 Mile.

But since a "Spork" is essentially a merged spoon and fork, you could, in essence, cut down on expenses by only buying "Sporks," ridding you of those meddlesome spoons and forks to clean, and lengthening your downloading time, or tv watching time, or whatever it is you like to do to relax in the evenings. Even though this may be simpler, I urge the citizens of our fine globe not to fall to this unesteemed low, and keep your spoons and forks, because lack of diversity is what will bring us to a world such as that in George Orwell's "1984," where the number of words is cut down each year, rather than expanding as any good one should. If you don't want our planet to drop to that low standard, use your "Sporks," and have a "Quickie," but don't blame me when there are no more spoons or forks!...

*An implement, i.e. one part of cutlery.

#108457 3 2005-10-15T11:23:23Z KinGAleX
Teh Review:

00-0 Desolace - Amazing tileset; vaguely reminded me of a few Metanet levels, but not in a bad way. Optimally placed rockets. The right amount of gold. Seriously, I worship you and your maps.

00-1 Wounded - Once again, amazing tileset: I don't even think I need to bother mentioning that any more. As a rule, I don't like chainguns, but somehow you pulled it off.

00-2 Patronage - Great map. Makes you think before you jump or whatever. A bit minimal, but still good.

00-3 Circumscribed - Almost certainly inspired by Toshosoft's map from his Episode. I can't remember the name. Wider than his was, and slightly easier. The floorguard added some annoyance though - once again, in a good way.

00-4 Return of the Native - This level screams "CLASSIC PITCH!" Very reminiscent of your earlier maps, and just as good. The exit switch was hard to get, and the gausses well placed.

The Package - Teh Rox0r - As close to perfect as you could get. There's just something about your maps that I love. Keep it up!

#117199 2 2005-11-23T05:22:30Z KinGAleX
You unlock a different colour, or "flavour", with each column you defeat. With the first column you defeat (it can be any) you unlock pink, and so on and so forth. No matter what order you beat the columns in, you'll get the flavours in the same order.
#117203 3 2005-11-23T05:45:28Z KinGAleX
If you put a map in your userlevels file which has many weird characters in the title, that can happen. Say someone has a map called "Check out these characters #$#@$%^@@#$!@#$". N will read those $s as the beginning of a new map, and will then nullify everything after it. Try renaming any maps with weird characters like that, or else it's something else.

#117598 7 2005-11-24T13:57:50Z KinGAleX
Yep. Follow the following path in your directory.

C:\Documents and Settings\<your login here>\Application Data\Macromedia\Flash Player\#SharedObjects\<random numbers and letters>\localhost

In that folder, there should be a file called, "n_v14b_userdata". Copy that file to your memory stick, and paste it in the same place you took it from on your school computer.

#5366 1 2005-11-25T01:51:40Z KinGAleX
THE FUN CONTEST

It's incredibly simple. All you folks have to do is create a map which is FUN! It can be any style of map, other than DDA, as long as it's FUN. (It doesn't actually have to be fun, but you'll get a better score that way...)

We're only going to judge you on three categories.
Funness of Tileset: This basically means how much fun your tileset allows us to have. One with a Playpark-style tileset will probably score alot of points in this section.
Funness of Objects: This means how much fun the objects allow us to have. Say, for instance, you have mines underneath each bounceblock. That's not very fun, and will get you rated down.
Funness as a Whole: This means the overall feel and funness of the map. Obviously.

All categories are out of 30. This gives a total out of 90 from each judge, and out of 270 overall.

The judges are:
blazeraid
KinGAleX
Templex
maestro (Backup)

If any judge enters a map, maestro will take that judge's place for the map.

All maps must be posted to Numa and linked directly to in this thread. I will then make the necessary adjustments to this post.

There is a limit of four maps per entrant, and you can bring a map up from any time. However, for each old map you enter, you must also enter a new map; but they don't have to be in any order, as long as they're both there.

You may not edit or replace any of your entries once they have been judged, or even partially judged.

All entries must be in by 11:59:59 pm (GMT+0) on the 17th of December.

Here are some examples for the contest:
Really pretty quite sorta fast by Formica
Seriously, just play that map. Flying around a circuit really quickly? Diagnosis = Fun

Shrapnel by tktktk
This one maintains a good flow, and it's hard to believe how awesomely it turns out. Diagnosis = Fun

Escalator To Heck by Niccus
However nice a map design this is, it's just not that fun. It's so frustrating! Diagnosis = Unfun

I'm not saying that you have to have easy racey levels to win this; those are just a couple which quickly come to mind.

[total] :: 63/90

Actually Guiseppi, that's not always the case. Your use of that question mark in the sentence, "I could care less?" can be wrong, since the thing you are quoting did not contain a question mark in the first place, therefore the quote itself is not a question. #123417 28 2005-12-16T10:49:26Z KinGAleX
Beo, please don't make all of Australia seem like one town.

In Brisbane, where I am, 'Footy' or 'Football' generally refers to the NRL, or Rugby League.
'Aussie Rules' or 'AFL' is the Australian Rules Football, or AFL.
'Rugby' or 'Union' is usually Rugby Union and the Reds.
'Grid-iron' refers to American Football.
'Soccer' does indeed mean the one original football. I know quite a few people who just call it 'Football' though.

#129826 5 2006-01-05T03:26:00Z KinGAleX
Deleting sniped maps doesn't do anything. It just means that the sniper wins. Do you really want to noobs to win?

Also, your idea of members not being able to vote until they make two maps with an average of at least 3.5 would definitely not work. The amount of overrated maps at the moment is horrendous, so 3.5 wouldn't show off any sort of skill.

As for the original idea, I quite like it, but I'm pretty sure that there are some people out there who haven't made many maps at all, or have a relatively low average rating, and have been rating maps for a long time. Take me as an abstract example. I've only made twenty-something maps, but I've been rating for about five months. I'm pretty sure I have enough voting expertise to allow the same amount of influence as, say, someone with a higher average rating, such as Baraba, or atob.

The most obvious reaction to that would be making the amount of influence parallel to the time spent at Numa. Once again, there could be people who have been here a long time, but still have terrible judgement on maps.

I reckon that this is a good idea in theory, but I doubt it would work to everyone's liking if implemented.

#130668 12 2006-01-08T03:11:20Z KinGAleX
wedgie, how can you say "as you only live once"? You may believe that, and can state that you believe it, but there's no way you'll know until it doesn't matter to you anyway.

Regardless of how many times you live, I do not believe that the death penalty should exist. It's one thing to put someone in jail for life, but to take their life? There's nothing higher than that, in my eyes.

A life in prison promotes change. The person goes through the hardships of that length of time spent in a controlled environment. At least they have a chance. What do people think killing criminals is going to do for the morale of the country?

The state: "By killing John Doe, we're showing the rest of the state what will happen if they do what we believe to be punishable by ending your life."

It all comes down to fear. People obviously aren't going to go out on murderous rampages if they know they're going to die for it. Fear isn't unnatural, but it's often not very healthy.

Innocent, guilty, I don't care much. I care that people are being killed against their own free will by their government, in exactly the same fashion as the government does so to other countries.

P.s. I didn't want to touch upon any religious aspect, as I don't really believe that comes into this.

Since we're on the topic of music in a musical topic, has anyone heard Sufjan Stevens' latest album, Illinois? I guess I can only really truly discuss it if anyone else here has heard it, but I mostly wanted to point out that it's really fantastic.
Agreed! If you liked the style of music in Illinois, you'd also like his other "state" album, Michigan. It's less theatrical (for lack of a better word), but all the stronger for it. My favourite of his albums. *points to avatar*

Right now, I'm really into the Flaming Lips forthcoming album, "At War With the Mystics". It's got a definite Yoshimi-vibe, which isn't a bad thing at all, seeing as how "Yoshimi Battles the Pink Robots" was probably their best album. Look for the songs 'The W.A.N.D (The Will Always Negates Defeat)' and the song 'Free Radicals (A Hallucination of the Christmas Skeleton Pleading With a Suicide Bomber)'. Their first single, 'Mr. Ambulance Driver' is already out, I believe. The video is here. [Note: QT link, dancing]

I'm really looking forward to this album, as well as the Yeah Yeah Yeah's new album. I haven't been able to find any tracks, except for their first single, 'Gold Lion'. The video can be found here. [Note: WMP link, guitar smashing]. It sounds less 'traditional' and a little more polished than their last LP, which I hope works out. I really want to like this album.

And I'll give you one more video by a band called Vitalic. This song is from last year's record, OK Cowboy. It's got some neat synth stuff, kinda dark. It's called 'Poney, Pt. 1', and the video is here. [Note: QT link, dogs]. The video is compellingly odd; sinister music juxtaposed with cute dogs being filmed in reverse/slo-mo. If you liked that, check out their song 'My Friend Dario' which has one of the best danceable guitar choruses in recent memory.

[edit] Does anyone have any objections to me resurrecting my music video thread from aeons ago? I'll take silence as a negative

#164182 13 2006-04-12T14:19:20Z KinGAleX
I'm not paranoid about my ratings at all. I only rate maps that look like I even want to bother playing them, and my average vote is about a 3.

I find some jumper puzzles to be really enjoyable. Keyword: some. The majority of them are badly thought-out, and provide nowhere near enough fun to warrant completion. Er, in conclusion, when done well, the jumper genre is one of my favourites, but as it is not done well enough often enough, they do tend to be overrated. Rating fairly is fun.

#165077 30 2006-04-15T15:40:13Z KinGAleX
Alright. I'm a'jumpin' on the bandwagon with my Top Ten Yes songs ever.

10 - Yours is No Disgrace Almost a standard rock song!
9 - I See You The first of three covers on this list. A slick reworking of a Byrds song.
8 - Something's Coming The second cover. Interestingly, it's a song from a musical.
7 - The Clap A Steve Howe solo piece. Effing brillo.
6 - Roundabout The most commercially famous Yes song, after Owner of a Lonely Heart.
5 - Everydays Contrast, contrast, contrast.
4 - Sound Chaser The funkiest part of my favourite album, Relayer.
3 - Lightning Strikes The only good song Yes wrote after 1974.
2 - America My favourite cover ever. Five-minute guitar solo in the middle of a Paul Simon song.
1 - Siberian Khatru In contention for my favourite song ever, and the one that got me into Yes.

I guess this list won't mean a lot to many people, but do note that all these songs are from albums between 1969 and 1974, excluding Lightning Strikes.

#192410 11 2006-06-21T08:29:32Z KinGAleX
Here's a short bit of prose entitled "The Cleaner":

She was depressed, and started to clean. It gave her a sense of power; control. Nothing made her feel better than to rid her flat of invisible scourges. Flat: boring; drab; not what she wanted. Her music rose to a plateau of warm sound; the dust shattered from the mantelpiece. In the docile silence, she played an inwards game of musical chairs. Not a sound did she make, nor movement. Again, a powerful surge of melody keeled her over like a moving wall, and she continued to clean.

And here, a lyrical poem of mine, in the most common sense of the word, entitled "Again":

Dockside, portside, rolling barrels,
Sailors gnash on hide-like marrow,
Captain yells, merchant sells,
Things are right, once more, again.

The door, not open, swings back-and-forth,
The needle swings justly ahead, due North,
Tolling bells, contents of wells,
Things are left askew, my friend.

The masts crash down, among the brethren,
Slaves below preach benevolent,
Care-takers, not candlestick makers,
Things are running around the bend.

Exhausted men refrain from sleep,
While sons and daughters begin to weep,
Promise breakers, zero takers,
Things are almost scraping the end.

The birds are gone, never a good sign,
The waters quelling, no longer benign,
All are tired, some admired,
Things are hopefully on the mend.

One pulls out a deck of cards,
The Queen of Hearts horribly marred,
No longer ired, the seas less wired,
Things are almost calm, say when.

Harbor close, birds back once more,
Times long past now jested in lore,
Satisfaction gels, say no to hells,
Things are right, once more, again.

#193881 166 2006-06-24T15:03:40Z KinGAleX
I'm not sure that Kashkin (Red_Reamer) has read this thread, so I'll post something on his behalf. It is a prime example of a heterosexual fighting for homosexual rights. He wrote a letter to a local paper in response to the letter of another. Here is the original letter (All names have been changed to preserve anonymity):

Quote: Not-Roslyn Phillips
The Advertiser, Friday, June 16, 2006
Timely intervention

  In 1934, Oxford anthropologist Professor J.D. Unwin published his research on the rise and fall of 80 of the worlds civilisations.
  He found that cultures which valued traditional marriage were creative and displayed great energy. However, when they rejected traditional marriage, including chastity before marriage and faithfulness afterwards, they did not survive more than two generations.
  Marriage in Western culture has been undermined for years. The ACT Government, by trying to treat homosexual relationships as pseudo-marriages, has been heading farther down this dangerous road. The Prime Minister deserves our thanks for his timely intervention.

And here, the response:

Quote: Kashkin
The Advertiser, Saturday, June 17, 2006
Recognition

  Thank you, Not-Roslyn Phillips (The Advertiser, 16/06/06), for a vague and misrepresentative argument; firstly, ancient Athenian society (from which we owe our political system of democracy) practised chastity and faithfulness with respect to marriage.
  But only for the women who were usually 13 when married off, men were free to enjoy concubines or hetaerae (companions; higher class sex workers).
  Despite having an imperfect marital system, Athens lasted far longer than two generations, and gave us ideas concerning philosophy, art and to an extent, politics. More importantly though, is the idea that allowing gay marriage will somehow undermine its current implementation, and harm society.
  There is a difference between accepting homosexuality, and encouraging it. I do not see how humanity, or Western Culture is at risk of extinction.
  Gay couples are damned unlikely to be producing children, whether married or not. I think too many people are voicing their own personal aversion by 'defending society.'
  Western Culture survived the abandonment of slavery; we will survive the recognition of the gay community.

#195749 2 2006-06-28T12:00:12Z KinGAleX
The equation I used was 0.1 x 2^50, thus giving 112 589 990 684 262.4 mm.

That's 112 589 990.6842624 km, or roughly one-hundred-and-twelve million kilometres.

One Astronomical Unit (AU) is the mean distance between the Earth and Sun, and is given (by Wikipedia) as "about one-hundred-and-fifty million kilometres", which is off of our estimate by about thirty-eight million kilometres.

Although it wouldn't actually reach the entire distance, at an exact thickness of 0.1 mm, that's pretty freaking thick, my friends.

#200381 5 2006-07-08T19:18:47Z KinGAleX
Hmm, that's not what I meant. Let me try again.

Quote: Guy
6. A massively multiplayer game must have a higher degree
a multiplayer-ness than a multiplayer game in order to be
"more massive."

Massively does not mean to be "more massive", therefore, the rest of his calculations are incorrect.

Also, multiplayer is a infinite number higher than one, as is massively multiplayer, so set Y needs to be the same as set X. As they are the same, Massively Multiplayer is not a logical idiocy, but a distinction that separates the likelihood of the number of players being in a higher part of the infinity > 1, from the likelihood of the number of players being in a lower part of the infinity > 1.

#11518 1 2006-10-01T06:36:12Z KinGAleX
For Windows users only.

Note: This also works with any other object that can possibly be shortcut to.

Thanks to LittleViking001 for finding this out for me.

Ever wanted to open N using the Run command? Well, here's how:

1. Create a Shortcut to your N .exe, and rename the Shortcut your desired input to the Run command.

eg. Mine is named 'n', so in Run, I type in 'n' to open it.

2. Copy (or cut) and paste this Shortcut into the folder C:\WINDOWS .

That's all there is to it.

The Run command is located in Start > Run... , or by pressing the Windows Key + R.

#236252 228 2006-10-02T05:51:08Z KinGAleX
I oppose uses of the words 'normal' and 'abnormal' simply because of their connotations. As I've said before, I prefer 'regular', or 'irregular', since they refer to how often this trait occurs.

I also think that your ball example, or any other example, is a subjective matter. When I think of the quintessential ball, I think of an ellipsoid and a spherical ball. Neither seems more 'normal' or 'regular' to me than the other.

In the same way, the quintessential human being to a person who has lived in Kenya their entire life might be a person with dark skin, whereas the quintessential human being to someone who has lived in a predominantly caucasian society their entire life might be fair-skinned.

Do note that in current society, however much we try to deny it, it's harder to further your species if you're of a minority.

#12406 1 2006-11-22T11:37:00Z KinGAleX
InsomNicon

The Contest:

The main objective is to stay awake for as long as possible, but there are a couple of conditions present:

Quote: Conditions!
You will need to be in the IRC for the duration, so that there's no cheating.

You will need to fulfil one task from the judges each hour, by the turn of the hour, or you will incur a penalty for the next hour. Some of these tasks will be N-related, others just plain stupid.

You may not sleep. Ever.

The last person awake wins!

Dave and I will be the judges, but if we feel we need a break, we might ask a respected fellow member to step in.

Information:

InsomNicon is not planned at the moment, but keep checking back. Players may join up to 4 hours after the start of the contest, however the hour-checkpoints missed will be considered when we determine the winner of the contest--therefore, if you showed up two hours late, you have to stick around for an extra two hours after other players have dropped out.

If you are at all interested in this, contact Dave or me ASAP, or leave a post in this thread.

Winners so far:

InsomNicon 1: Blizzard

Thanks guys!

Results of Round Seven

Sept - This carries a degree of irony because it's a stereotypical construction worker's lunchbox. No real person would own this lunchbox, unless that person was a construction worker.

Sucker - This lunchbox is too colourful to be carried non-ironically. It also includes a booklet that says "The Laptop Lunch", as well as an extremely ironic fork/spoon combo.

Maniac - This lunchbox is mildly unironic, but is extremely uninteresting. A person who would purchase this lunchbox probably does not have enough concept of irony at all to select a more unironic lunchbox than this one.

Blizzard - This lunchbox is only unironic in China or Mongolia. Here, it would be extremely ironic to possess such a lunchbox, because of its outward design. No matter how practical it is, its appearance gives it a level or irony.

SlappyMcGee - This lunchbox is by far the least ironic of the lunchboxes. This lunchbox is packed to the hinges with unirony.

Kablizzy - Kablizzy's lunchbox is so ironic, it's not even a lunchbox. It is instead a clever pop-culture pun taken from the game itself. YOU LOSE, SUCKA.

Most user-submitting sites (such as Youtube, or unfortunately more commonly, most porn sites) use tags to provide a link between certain files.

They're basically keywords added to a map that allow it to be searched for easily. For example, a map could have the tags "laser drone", "launchpad", "DDA", to make it easier to search for.

Servers have nothing to do with italics. /This kind of italic/ is Chatzilla specific. You can put formatting on text another way, but we don't like that in the channel.

KinGAleX is bored.

Any formatting placed on a /me command is client-specific.

Firstly, your idea is just terrible. 'Abstract' is not a style of map. The 'good kind' of 'Abstract' you speak of is just an interesting way of making maps. The bad kind is, as you did say, an annoying attempt at being cool.

Still, you cannot compare krusch and yahoozy in terms of style. That's a ridiculous idea.

Do remember that in artistic terms, abstractism is simply a non-traditional representation, using devices and objects somewhat different to regularity. You can't reduce something that broad to yahoozy, krusch, and Cerberus.

Also, your English is infuriating.

Challenge Seventeen

Hooray for fishy twists on popular games!

This challenge is an easy one to participate in. It's just a Stonefish, Anemone, Clownfish ladder contest. (Or Rock Paper Scissors, for those unenlightened.) Stonefish stings Clownfish, Anemone wraps Stonefish, Clownfish is impervious to Anemone.

All games will be best of three, moderated by me, where ties are replayed. All throws will be PMed to me, from where I will announce either the winner and throw, or tie and throw. List of games:

1. apg vs Blizzard
2. ChaosWolf vs fingolfin
3. George vs maestro
4. spect vs Sucker
5. Ultima vs unconditional
6. W1 v W2
7. W3 v W4
8. W5 v W1
9. W2 v W3
10. W4 v W5

At this point, any player with two wins advances to the final. The same sequence will be played again until two players are left on top with the most wins. A best of five match will be played between these two to determine the overall champion.

Two points for participating.

Three points for winning the ladder.

Good luck!

    #334282 8 2007-06-22T02:38:47Z KinGAleX
    Johnny the Homicidal Maniac is one of my favourite comic serieses. I applaud you for reading it. Jhonen was still great when he made Invader Zim, but JtHM and Squee are better.

    I don't think Johnny's mentality is supposed to be a representation of what we should be as animals, but a privileging of the dichotomy we find ourselves in when we try to embrace what is natural and what is man-made. Man-made is technically natural, since it's the result of natural processes, but in showing what we could be if we acted not only to further our own species but to eradicate what one person perceives as the weakest, I don't think he's trying to say that that is what you should be, rather that that is another way of looking at things - a more subjective way of looking at things - and that this perspective is equally valid.

    Either way, it's a fun comic, and an interesting philosophical journey. I strongly recommend it to everyone.

    #15847 1 2007-06-23T06:50:28Z KinGAleX
    I was lying in bed last night, and I can't remember why, but I came up with this idea. The idea runs thusly: a group of people, in the form of a book club, discussing an album each week, from the progressive rock or metal genres. And then I thought it'd be good over IRC. And then I thought there might be some interest from other people to either join in or read it. Therefore: The Prog Log.

    The discussion itself will take place among me, Kashkin, krusch, and a guest each week. These discussions will take place in #proglog on the IRC once a week. Each week is defined as the time from Saturday to Friday, since we're starting the first one on a Saturday. So, for instance, it could so happen that we do one on a Friday night, and then one the next night. Suffice to say, there will be one each week, where the week is between Saturday and Friday. Anyone is welcome to spectate, but will not be able to contribute.

    After each week's discussion is over, I'll be posting a transcript at The Prog Log Blog. You can go there to read the first one now!

    Well, there you go! That's all I've got to say, so here's the upcoming topic list:

    Week One: The Mars Volta - Frances the Mute. Guest appearance by spect.
    Saturday 23rd June 2007, 10:00 PM AEST (GMT+10)
    5:00 AM for West Coast America and 8:00 AM for East. 1:00 PM for the UK.

    Week Two: Transatlantic - Bridge Across Forever. Major Tom Kidd filling in for krusch. Guest appearance by yahoozy_Guacamole (Poulpe).
    Date and time TBD.

    Week Three: Tool - Lateralus. Major Tom Kidd filling in for krusch. Guest appearance TBD.
    Date and time TBD.

    Week Four: Opeth - Blackwater Park. Guest appearance by your favourite forumer, Clovic.
    Date and time TBD.

    Week Five: maudlin of the Well - Bath. Guest appearance by Borealis.
    Date and time TBC.

    #338733 35 2007-07-04T12:25:08Z KinGAleX
    My name really didn't have as conceited origins as you might think. The King refers to an ambigram I came up with at some point in time, for the word 'king'. It looked pretty cool, and I started using it because I made an avatar for it before I actually created any accounts for anything. Alex is my name.

    The pattern of lower-case and upper-case letters is meant to represent a crown, to go with the King theme, but no-one really picks up on that.

    #343163 6 2007-07-15T02:44:49Z KinGAleX
    Standouts: 11, 13, 14, 15. Strong ending.

    I really enjoyed the mix between a more contemporary style of mapping, and a Metanet-influenced 'classic' style. You didn't go overboard on (arguably) complex tilesets, but you definitely made the maps interesting and unique enough to ensure replay. I definitely felt strong Ngaged, mc_george, LittleViking, and Barabajagal influences in there, but maybe I'm just pulling at strings. I've played a lot of maps, and honestly, for your maps to be like, but distinct from, those of those authors, that's a pretty good bloody achievement.

    4.5/5 for the pack, buddy. I really enjoyed it.

    Unreality, I think your ideas are solid and all, but constructing themed columns to add on to the game sort of goes against the spirit of the game.     #369570 17 2007-10-04T06:01:46Z KinGAleX
    Here's my poem submission, entitled The Flower:

    Wisping lovelily, the gilded fireflies.
Down against the flow: an anti-drain
Of catastrophic proportions;
The sweetest are the thinnest in this inverted food pyramid.

If I were they, I think I'd be a nihilist.
And my brethren would soar with me, fully showered of vehemence.

That is silly.

The fireflies, fewer and still present, have in their minds set high stakes;
Probably not to win, but to waive darkness and perceive union
As it is
Though they do not know it.
Blindess will relinquish their hold on action,
Empower mediate senses, with the continued drilling experienced together.

Still, I would think nihilism the only way to go.

    Also, I think this competition would work better if poets and mapmakers teamed up. For example, if one map was made for each poem, as a pair. As I'm sure you know, the five poems you choose as the 'best' ones might not be seen as such by the competitors, and I think it would be a real help to the mapmakers if they could choose which poem they wanted to make a map for.

    #384778 116 2007-11-30T23:29:59Z KinGAleX
    Paddington

    To the man in the yellow suit wandering the hallways
    I say unto you verily, "Man, give me a smile"
    For your followers are weary standing militarily rank and file
    And from here whichever way you go they will follow you always

    "Here you come!" says the entroper, lifting a foot from the gravel
    Looking left, looking right, it is all just the same
    Be you viscount, countess, duke, earl, duchess or dame
    On November the fifth be away with your whip and hand some poor layman your gavel

    We walked for days behind the man in the yellow suit
    Clutching each others' hands as though we had nothing else to do
    Before he turned, gave a sigh, hesitated, turned again, and began anew
    While in this arid desert of life we searched for the fruit

    With our faces drowning and our backs in pain
    He shouted a command with no purpose, really, but it made us feel full
    And we exerted full force with minds and muscles - the push and the pull
    Finally, after darkness, we were thrown water again

    So I ran from the man in the yellow suit, the suit made of gold
    And I wrote him a letter on paper made of dead animal skins
    I wrote: "He who is wisest is the one who knows how to and wins
    While the novice remains nameless, for he is nothing but bold"

    I ran from the man to the preacher's home
    Living two days with the man of the word of God
    I was granted accomodation, food, wine and Jesus, for to listen and nod
    I treaded fearfully, nodded profusely, my mind did not roam

    To the man in the yellow suit wandering the heavens
    It is that which you make which makes that which is you
    And if you remember there is but nought you can do
    You'll have no more problems with your sixes, eights, and sevens, nines, tens, elevens
    You'll drop all your weapons
    Transgress to the shepherds
    I doubt you'll heal lepers but I won't stop you trying
    Attempting the waivers with sickening quavers
    A battle-born knave of celestial favours
    With your humility will come the peasants
    Craving attention for they are weak and you will turn them down without a headturning otherwise
    As long as there's no fear that we can see the black in your eyes
    The man in the yellow suit I salute you
    #384964 50 2007-12-01T11:31:30Z KinGAleX
    Uh, guys, it's a preference, not a strict evaluative test. If you're getting fifty fifty you're not taking the test as it's meant to be taken. 83% Introverted is the same as 4% Introverted, as far as the test and therefore the letters are concerned. We all have parts of our shadow functions in us: the aim of the test is to understand our preferences. If you actually believe you are fifty fifty Extroverted Introverted or any other dichotomy, one of those sides is based on some huge influence around you, not what you actually prefer.

    INTP, through the thick and thin.

    #18855 1 2008-01-16T00:07:55Z KinGAleX
    I came into the Legacy team, and participated in Zenith under the auspice that I was there for my creativity and artistry. It seems, in retrospect, this was a misconception.

    In the end, I made fourteen maps for Zenith, five of which were accepted, and four of those five edited. I hold no beef with any of the Legacy leaders, compilers, and editors for any of this, because I know that they just have different mindsets to me. I am entirely anti-Creative-Control, and for the artistry. Some of the other Legacy guys prefer commerciality and a sense of a conglomerate, as opposed to the parts of a whole. I felt a little slighted by some of the edits, but I do realise that this is just their ideologies as opposed to mine.

    And so, I present to you the unedited unadulterated maps I made for Zenith, as submitted to the Legacy team. I quite like them.

    Oh my lord I did not know you have a Custom 24, Bor. Totally my dream guitar.

    Anyway, I have a cheap-ish Fender acoustic that was my first guitar. No pickup, cost me about 500 bucks three years ago. So I've been playing about three years. I played that solely for about a year and a half, 'til I had the urge to play electric guitar, which I didn't 'til I got into Yes. I've pretty much moved on from that stage, in that I basically only play electric now. I digress.

    I bought a shitty Ibanez G-RG in about September last year as something to tide me over, but then I got a great deal on a vintage RG, an American-only model, probably from about the same time as Dave's. That was also 500, but I daresay worth more. It has a cute little chip near the bottom. It's got a 250 dollar Floydie on it and an Evo II bridge pickup. I quite like it.

    I suppose I should mention I use Ernie Ball nickel wound 11 to 48s.

    As I said before, my dream guitar is a Custom 24, so hopefully some day ...

    I use a Boss OD-3 for overdrive / distortion, and a Line 6 compression pedal for a sweet funky ass sound. I'd also like to grab a Line 6 pod, but I've so many things to save for it'll probably be a long time coming.

    I've got a pretty crappy amp that I got for free from my dad, 60W, but it's got dual input, which is pretty wicked. Don't care much about choosing an amp. Vintage Fender Twin my mate's got is pretty nice, but I'll probably end up with a Mesa Boogie or a Orange stack.

    Cool.
